As for the distinctive fists: the fist of the vajra bond resting on the two thumbs is the pledge fist; made twofold, it is the tathāgata fist. With the two tathāgata fists and the five circles one should prostrate and enter. These too grant all accomplishments.

Next, the binding of the karma mudrās. With the supreme vajra body, placing it at the crown of the head, at the ūrṇā, and at the heart, and circling it round and round, it should be held at the cheeks and at the uṣṇīṣa. With the two index fingers of the two tathāgata fists one makes the threatening forefinger and the gesture of giving. Holding the left at the heart, one touches the ground with one finger of the right. With the wrathful index finger of the left turned inward toward the right fist: this is the fist of all the tathāgatas, by which buddhahood is swiftly attained. Bound below the vajra bond, it is the firm cross-legged posture.

Next, the tantra of the extensive rite of mudrās of the Vajra family. The mudrā of entering is just the summoning mudrā of the wrathful vajra of supreme joy. The great mudrās are likewise. The pledge mudrā: below, with the vajra bond, all the fingers blazing, and the thumb vajra within, is Vajrakrodha, tirintiri, which accomplishes the great Vajradhara. The others are likewise. Those of the Dharma are likewise, and the karma mudrās are likewise. As for the distinctive fist: with the vajra fist, the index finger is the threatening forefinger. Those that arise from the pledge are likewise. Next, the karma mudrās arise from Vajrahūṃkāra. With the little fingers of the vajra hands bound like a bracelet, the threatening forefinger is Vajrahūṃkāra, standing with the left leg extended. The vajra threatening forefinger is made with the index finger of the left. It is done with the breath of the nose and the syllable hūṃ. In wrath one should join to it the syllable a. One should laugh aloud with the laughter ha ha. By meditating on a vajra at the heart, it is the great mudrā of the vajra.

Next, the tantra of the extensive rite of mudrās of the Lotus family. Bending the two index fingers of the tathāgata vajra and placing it at the heart is the vajra lotus. All the mudrās, the great mudrā and the rest, are likewise. Then the distinctive pledge mudrā and the distinctive fist. From the palms joined like a bud, opening the thumbs and the little fingers forms the great four-petaled lotus. All the bindings are just as before. As for the distinctive fist: with the vajra bond, the two index fingers are bent, the thumbs are extended evenly, and the two middle fingers are placed side by side facing outward; made twofold, this is the lotus fist. Next, the karma mudrās. This is the explanation of the great lotus. That same is held at the crown of the head. With that same, one should gaze with the vajra gaze. That same is made with all the fingers opened. That same forms the eyes of the vajras, two by two. That same makes the gesture of approval. Placing that same at the forehead, one should make supplication. That same, the sign of the thumb, is inserted into the left womb.

Next, the extensive rite of the tantra of mudrās of the Jewel family. Fully extending the vajra palms, placing the bent index fingers on the even thumbs, and setting it at the place of the ūrṇā on the forehead, one bestows all the empowerments. The binding of all the mudrās, the great mudrā and the rest, is likewise. The pledge mudrā: with the vajra bond, the two index fingers bent outward and the fingers even, the jewel enclosed by the thumb vajra, is the vajra jewel. The others are the vajra jewel fist that arises from it. Made twofold, it is the jewel fist. Next, the karma mudrās. The raised vajra jewel is the sprout to be accomplished. With the palms joined and the ring fingers and little fingers opened evenly, it holds the supreme silk streamer. The middle fingers form the jewel. The tips of the little fingers. Scattering below. From the vajra bond, with the jewel of the index fingers extended, it is the garland. The three vajra jewels should be bound in front and on the two sides. Placing the vajra jewel at the place of the ūrṇā, with the tips of the index fingers one makes a knot above in the manner of silk streamers. Making the two thumbs into the firm cross-legged posture, bending the two index fingers, even and shaped like a bud, is the one with the supreme body, the supreme body of all the tathāgatas. With it, beginning from the crown of the head, having performed the action in front, on the right, on the left, and behind, and likewise in front, behind, and on the two sides, with graceful movement, one releases the last fingers and the rest, makes the knot that binds the garland, and forms it in the manner of silk streamers. This is the empowerment of Vajrasattva, and by it one attains the very state of Vajrasattva, supreme among all the lords of the three realms of existence. All the rites of all the vajras are just as above.

Next, this is what is to be known as the outer mudrās. The great mudrā is the mudrā with a bowl in the left hand and, in the right, the shooting of an arrow.

The pledge mudrā of entering: the index, middle, and ring fingers are joined well and evenly to form three points, and the two thumbs and little fingers are linked like a chain. This is the mudrā of the protectors. Held in just the same way, it is the mudrā of the goddesses. The pledge mudrā: with the middle and ring fingers in the vajra bond, the index, middle, little, and ring fingers are gathered at the root of the thumb. This is the mudrā of the maṇḍala of the four sisters. If one is unable to prepare the maṇḍala, then by binding this the maṇḍala will be fully prepared, and accomplishments too are fully bestowed. With this very mudrā the goddesses should be invited and made to enter.

Next, the wheel of illusion: on the left hand all the fingers are fully extended. It is the wheel of the pledge maṇḍala, which makes all the illusions of all the asuras effective. With the two thumbs of the lord of the hooded serpents, and with the eight fingers whose tips are turned outward forming a mass of hoods, it is the pledge for summoning, bringing in, binding, and bringing under control all the nāgas. This is the hand of the mahoraga. If one transgresses it, one will die. Knotting the four, the middle and ring fingers, raising them upward in a gesture of delight and drawing them together, if one strikes with the tips of the two little fingers, all poisons blaze; and binding all poisons, gathering them from everywhere and striking, all poisons are destroyed. With this very mudrā one should render free of poison all poison that has been eaten and all that has spread. This is the tantra of the extensive rite of the outer mudrās.

Then the Blessed One, the vajra secret of Great Bliss, the great pledge, Vajrasattva, said, "Next I shall explain the extensive rite of the tantra of mudrās that accomplish all rites."

Of these, the five mudrās of body are as follows. The great mudrā is for the family of the great Vajradhara. The pledge mudrā is for the Tathāgata family. Vajrahūṃkāra is for the Vajra family. The great lotus is for the Lotus family. The great vajra jewel is for the Jewel family.

Next, the five mudrās of speech. Singing with every flow of song, in whatever way one is able, in all the styles of every kind of melodious song, and reciting: this is the vajra speech of supreme joy, for the family of Vajrasattva. The vajra speech in which the tongue and the two lips are utterly unmoving, their surfaces not touching at all, and which is free of melody, is for the Tathāgata family; it is needed by all the families. That which has the thunder of clouds together with the subtle sound of the syllable hūṃ is the speech called the vajra of Vajrakrodha, for the Vajra family. The speech of meditative absorption is for the Lotus family, and is called the lotus that purifies all. The speech of Dharma is for the Jewel family, and is the jewel of offering.

Next, the five mudrās of mind. For accomplishing everything, it is by the very being of Vajrasattva. By recollection of the Buddha, it is for the Tathāgata family. The thought, "It shall be slain, by me or before me," is for the Vajra family. The thought, "It shall be purified," is for the Lotus family. By meditating on the whole of space, it is for the Jewel family.

Next, the five mudrās of the vajra. The five-pointed vajra whose points are wholly pervaded by a garland of light rays, with the vajra fist of Great Bliss, is for the family of Vajrasattva. The five-pointed vajra with light rays, with the tathāgata fist, is for the Tathāgata family. The four-pointed vajra, blazing in nature, with the fist of Vajrakrodha, is for the Vajra family. The tathāgata vajra upon a lotus, with the vajra lotus fist, is for the Lotus family. The half vajra, the vajra jewel, with the jewel fist, is for the Jewel family.

Then the Blessed One, the Unfailing and Unimpeded, possessor of swift and supreme accomplishment, Vajrasattva, said, "Next I shall explain the tantra of all practices, the extensive rite of swift and supreme accomplishment: the secret that comprehends all the families, the unimpeded and unfailing truth of reality."

The great mudrā of Vajrasattva is the secret pledge of the vajra of Great Bliss. The mudrā of speech is the heart mantra. The mudrā of mind is the near-heart mantra. The mudrā of the vajra is that of Vajragarvā.

Next, this is the unfailing and unimpeded tantra of the subtle karma mudrās. Holding the lower lip with the teeth and gazing with the vajra gaze, one experiences, each in an instant, the accomplishments of passion for all, of bringing under control, of play, and of making firm: this is for the family of Vajrasattva. Blessing it with the subtle vajra and gazing merely as a pledge, one attains the accomplishment of beholding the moral discipline, meditative absorption, wisdom, liberation, and the wisdom of liberation of all the tathāgatas: this arises from the Tathāgata family. With a wrathful frown, as a wrathful one, making a fierce gaze and holding it utterly unwavering, the accomplishments of felling all, bringing down, paralyzing, putting to sleep, sealing, killing, stupefying the armies of others, and complete victory over all arise in an instant: this is for the family of the wrathful vajra fist. With the steady vajra gaze, the eyes not closed, and the lips even, the accomplishments of purifying all obscurations, of protection, and of the manifestation of meditative absorption arise: this is for the Lotus family. With a blazing gaze and the face broadly smiling, one swiftly attains the accomplishments of all empowerments, of all excellence, and of the extensive rites of offering.

Next, the practice of binding all the mudrās:

Once each day, openly,
One practices one's own heart mantra;
Binding them oneself, in brief,
All the mudrās will be accomplished.

Next, the secret practice of all the mudrās:

This practice of the heart mudrā
Of the vidyā mantra is marvelous.
By reciting as much as one can and as much as one wishes,
The near-heart mantra is the secret.

The unfailing and unimpeded secret,
And likewise the accomplishment of one's own nature:
Even for those who are without merit,
Every accomplishment will be achieved.
To those who have not entered the maṇḍala
This supreme heart mantra should not be given,
Nor the secret, nor the sublime secret tantra,
If one desires delightful accomplishment.
In whatever house it comes to dwell,
This mudrā and mantra is auspicious;
Others cannot overcome it or harm it,
And all accomplishments will be attained.

The second section, the root tantra of the Mantra Section, is complete.

For the happiness of all sentient beings,
And to accomplish all that is to be accomplished,
All the buddhas together with their heirs
Made request of the holder of the supreme vajra:
"For the thorough accomplishment of great bliss
And for the purification of great passion,
O Great Being, please explain
The tantra that gathers all the rites.
For phenomena that are free of concepts,
What is it here that is called a rite?
What is it that is called the heart,
And what is the tantra of wisdom like?
What is it that is called a maṇḍala?
What qualities come with entering it?
What are the distinctions of the wheels of the families,
And what, moreover, is the practice like?
Vajrasattva, the very being,
How is he, and what is his reality?
Being without beginning or end,
And how supreme joy comes about;
Whose is this first heart mantra?
And what is suchness?
By whom was the song of hūṃ sung,
And how is its meaning to be told?
The mind as the vajra itself,
And likewise the mudrā as the vajra itself;
The arising of the buddhas,
And likewise of the families too;
The accomplishing of the buddhas' awakening,
And how buddhas become māras;
The accomplishing of the rites as well,
The reality of the first heart mantra,
The practice of the heart mantra, the mudrā, and the vidyā mantra,
And likewise the accomplishments:
How are they achieved by those without merit?
How does one become a master?
Flowers, incense, and lamps,
And scents: what are they, and how do they come about?
How, too, is offering to be made?
What is the secret offering?
Those who do all things, who eat all things, the sinful,
The utterly sinful: how do they reach accomplishment?
Through the unfailing and unimpeded accomplishment,
How do they become accomplished?
And what, moreover, is that secret?
By what is it accomplished in this very life?
For the thorough accomplishment of great bliss
And for the purification of great passion,
The secret of the reality of the secret:
Please explain it all to us."

Then the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, having heard the words of request made by all the tathāgatas, smiled broadly, and with a great laugh performed the subjugation called the vajra of the reality of all the great pledges. Then he spoke this subsequent tantra of all the rites.

Of these, the tantra of the pervasion of nonconceptuality:

From the meditative absorption free of concepts
The welfare of beings is thoroughly considered.
Therefore it is explained as a rite,
For conceptual thought is completely purified.

Of these, this is the tantra of the maṇḍala of the vajra secret of Great Bliss:

Having drawn the places of the letters on the ground,
One recites "Great Bliss."
Its pledge is the vajra,
Which accomplishes all the heart mantras.

Of these, the tantra of the explanation of the maṇḍala:

According to the place and according to the time,
According to the world and its nature,
The retinue is to be arranged;
Draw the maṇḍala in that manner.

Next, the tantra of the ripening of having entered all the maṇḍalas:

Whatever merit there is in entering
The maṇḍala at that time
With Vajrasattva and the other beings
Together with their retinues,
Drawing it in that way is just the same.

Next, the tantra of the distinctions of the wheels of all the families:

As the vajra wheel has been taught,
So is the sublime iron wheel,
And likewise the blazing wheel,
The eight-petaled lotus, and the great jewel.

Next, the tantra of accomplishing all the maṇḍalas:

Having prepared the maṇḍala,
One should enter by the inner sequence;
At the maṇḍalas one should recite
jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ prasiddhyamaṃ.

Next, the tantra of Vajrasattva himself:

Of all beings, the buddhas and the rest,
He is the self of all, the supreme Blessed One.
Because beings arise from him, he is the being,
Renowned as Vajrasattva.

Next, the tantra explaining the reality of the great pledge:

The being without beginning or end
Is Vajrasattva, great joy,
Samantabhadra, the self of all,
And likewise saṃsāra and nirvāṇa.

Next, the tantra explaining the reality that is without beginning or end:

All that is without beginning or end
Is the abode of space, the great element.
Because the arising of space is nonarising,
Vajrasattva is just the same.

Next, the tantra explaining the supreme joy of the Blessed One:

Because of supreme joy there is affliction;
All beings desire happiness.
In order to purify that happiness,
The Blessed One arose before the three worlds.

Next, the tantra explaining the reality of the first heart mantra:

Passion for all, great passion,
Awakens all who are possessed of desire.
The heart of the gandharva of blazing passion
Is Vajrapāṇi.

Next, the tantra of singing the song of the syllable hūṃ:

From song comes passion, and from joy, desire;
From supreme joy all things arise.
Because all is accomplished as arising,
The Blessed One spoke it in the first eon.

Next, the tantra explaining the meaning of the recitation of the syllable hūṃ:

By swiftly drawing all things with passion,
All accomplishments are drawn in passion.
The supreme means of drawing with passion
Is the meaning of reciting the heart mantra.

Next, the tantra of the vajra mind:

Just as songs and the wrathful one,
When gathered, enter everywhere,
The vajra thunderbolt becomes firm;
Likewise is the vajra gathered.

Next, the tantra on knowing the arising of Vajrasattva:

The being who is the self of all
Abides also in the self of oneself;
Vajrasattva, in actuality,
Swiftly enters into all as well.

Next, the tantra on knowing the arising of the Tathāgata:

From desire comes passion, from joy comes bliss;
From bliss comes sin, from sin comes suffering;
From suffering comes utterly firm meditative absorption;
From the vajra itself the buddhas arise.

Next, the tantra on knowing the arising of the Tathāgata family and the rest:

That very Tathāgata family
Is itself the great Vajra family;
It is itself the pure Lotus family,
And itself is called the supreme Jewel family.

Next, the tantra on knowing full awakening:

Discerning one's own mind, each aspect in turn,
The mind of awakening truly arises.
Because one is the vajra itself, one is the Buddha himself:
Meditate intently on oneself as the Buddha.

The tantra on knowing the Tathāgata as Māra himself:

One's own mind itself is Māra;
Mind itself is the Tathāgata.
In order to tame one's own mind,
The Buddha arises as Māra himself.

Next, the tantra on knowing the accomplishment of all the rites:

Whatever is to be accomplished in all the rites,
Whether it moves or does not move,
Through the yoga of one's own deity
All of these are to be accomplished.

Next, the tantra on knowing the practice of the first heart mantra:

By the power of knowing the meaning of recitation,
One fully accomplishes Vajrasattva.
The supreme recitation, for as long as one wishes,
Bestows the bliss of great accomplishment.

Next, the tantra on knowing the practice of the heart mantra, the mudrā, the mantra, and the vidyā:

Whatever being's heart mantra,
Mudrā, mantra, and vidyā there are,
For the purpose of recitation, the great being,
Oneself, is what is to be accomplished.

Next, the tantra on knowing the accomplishments:

The great accomplishment of Vajrasattva,
And likewise that of all the tathāgatas:
Vajrasattva is the very being,
For the sake of the supreme accomplishment of all acts.

Next, the tantra on knowing how accomplishment is achieved by those without merit:

Having drawn whatever wheel one wishes,
Even with ash or with anything at all,
And having set the great being upon it,
One should practice for four days and nights.

Next, the tantra on knowing the attainment of the state of master:

In the city of the pledge,
The supreme secret of the vajra of Great Bliss,
One should oneself fully enter as is proper,
And be empowered with the water of nectar.

Next, the tantra on knowing the presentation of offerings such as flowers:

All the offerings to the Vajra Holder
Are white for the Tathāgata family;
For the Vajra itself they are black;
Red for the Lotus, and yellow for the Jewel.

Next, the tantra on knowing the presentation:

Whether complete or incomplete,
Or else just as they are obtained,
Through offerings such as the vajra dance
All things will always be accomplished.

Next, the tantra on knowing the timely offerings:

With melodious songs, the ṣaḍja note and the rest,
And with all songs, as one wishes,
Vajra Opener and the others are set free;
The offerings should be fully made.

Next, the tantra on knowing the secret offering:

The offerings together with the vajra dance,
In their proper sequence,
Even if not spoken with the meaning of recitation,
Will bring about the supreme accomplishment.

Next, the tantra on knowing how even those who commit great sins attain accomplishment:

Even without having entered the maṇḍala,
Those of broken moral discipline and the like,
By the power of meditative absorption,
Will be freed from all sins.

Next, the tantra on knowing the cause of not attaining accomplishment:

Passion is not to be disparaged;
It is pure, and likewise it is bliss.
The Blessed One too is great passion.
Because this is not pure, accomplishment does not come.

Next, the tantra on knowing the practice of the supreme accomplishment:

Having become endowed with the proper object,
One should practice the supreme accomplishment.
This yoga, in all the rites,
Brings accomplishment in this very life.

This is the extensive rite of the subsequent tantra of all the rites, from the Precious Vajra Jewel, the great king of rites of the vajra secret of Great Bliss.

Then all the tathāgatas, pervading all the emanations of the realm of phenomena to the very limit of the realm of space, made manifestly known the wheel of the Blessed One, the rite that is the vajra secret of all the tathāgatas, the vajra jewel of the empowerment of all the tathāgatas, the vajra lotus of the lord of wisdom who beholds the rites of all the tathāgatas, the extensive rite of accomplishment of the firm vajra realm, which by the vajra of the extensive rite of sublime activity, through the various deeds of all the tathāgatas, pervades all without exception and without remainder. Having done so, they spoke this tantra of the practice of the vajra of the secret reality of the first heart mantra:

As long as they have entered the state of saṃsāra,
So long do they deliver sentient beings.
The glorious one who abides in nirvāṇa
Is not able to work for the welfare of beings.
Therefore the sublime beings
Enter saṃsāra for as long as saṃsāra lasts,
Wholly intent on the welfare of beings.
Possessed of the supreme quality of total purity,
Since the tathāgatas do not tame them,
They do not work for the welfare of beings.
Here is the supreme accomplishment, fully accomplished,
At all times and in every way.
The great Vajradhara and the others,
In this way, through the ripening of merit,
Through being seen by all and the like,
Do not act by way of their own karma.
Even without having entered the maṇḍala,
One will swiftly accomplish it in this existence.
Through being clearly seen and the like,
They grant it fully to all sentient beings;
Everywhere, at all times, to all,
All accomplishments will be fully granted.
The king, the great Vajradhara,
Is chief of all the lords of existence;
Therefore this practice is to be explained,
The accomplishment that is utterly permanent,
By which, in this very existence,
One attains the utterly permanent accomplishment.
Whether cast or hammered,
Painted as a picture, or fashioned,
The image of Vajrapāṇi
Should be fully installed as explained.

This is the extensive rite of accomplishment of the practice of the reality of the pledge of the vajra secret of Great Bliss.

Then the Blessed One, all the tathāgatas, through the wisdom of mudrā, again spoke this tantra of accomplishment of the practice of all the mudrās.

Each day, beforehand, at the proper time,
The first vajra, great bliss:
Through this, at all times,
By gathering all the mudrās in brief,
Binding the pledge correctly, one accomplishes;
Thereafter one is accomplished without having to practice.

Then the Blessed One spoke this practice of the secret mantra of the reality of Vajradharma:

Whether standing or sitting,
Or remaining wherever one may be,
Through the yoga of passion for all,
All accomplishments will be attained.

Then the Blessed One spoke this tantra of the secret reality of action:

Making offering with the supreme secret dance,
Or else making offering as one pleases,
If one then practices the Protector,
One swiftly attains the supreme accomplishment.

Next, the tantra of practicing oneself through one's own mudrā:

Placing the great mudrā in equipoise,
Practice once with one's own heart mantra.
Steady, utterly delighted, passionate toward all:
Utterly delighted, the Lord is accomplished.

Next, the tantra of accomplishment by singing the song of the syllable hūṃ:

Adorned with ri bhi da and the rest,
As much as one can and as one wishes,
Having sung the first gandharva song,
One is accomplished through the syllable hūṃ.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the first of great passion, Vajrasattva, the great Vajradhara. This is the tantra of practicing the secret accomplishment of the great reality.

Then the Blessed One, Vajrasattva, whose activity is without beginning or end, said, "Next I shall explain the karma mudrās of all the rites."

Increase of a very long life is through the Vajradharma of Great Bliss. Increase of a very youthful age is through the vajra elixir of Great Bliss. Increase of great beauty and great splendor is through the splendor of the vajra form of Great Bliss. Increase of all great formations is through the great vajra formation of Great Bliss. Protection of all the selves of all sentient beings is through the vajra protection of Great Bliss. Binding all the directions, the intermediate directions, and the maṇḍala is through the vajra encircling of Great Bliss; then even Vajra, the great king of wrathful ones, whose nature is the great vajra of all, will be unable to enter it, and it will be unbreakable. Protection from all sins is through the vajra blessing of Great Bliss. Making all one's own accomplishments, or those of others, into vajra is through the vajra armor of Great Bliss. Victory in all battles is through the armlet of the supreme vajra banner of Great Bliss. Protection in battle is through the invincibility of Great Bliss. Destroying all the armies of others is through the vajra holding of the vajra of Great Bliss. The defeat, rising, falling, and slaying of the armies of others is through the vajra lightning circle of Great Bliss. Pacifying all diseases, fevers and the rest, and great epidemics is through the vajra seizing of Great Bliss. Destroying all poisons and rendering them harmless is through the action of the vajra mudrā of Great Bliss. Freeing from all grahas is through the vajra expulsion of bhūtas of Great Bliss. The great freeing from mātṛkās, ḍākinīs, and all grahas, their destruction, drawing out the hearts of others, reviving the dead, increasing life, and utterly protecting it, are through the action of the vajra mudrā of Great Bliss. Bringing down possession on all, making them tremble, binding them, making them dance, making them sing, making former things appear and establishing each one, marvels, displaying miracles, making all speak every truth, releasing, protecting, and reviving, are through the vajra possession of Great Bliss. Accomplishing every prasena divination one wishes for, on all things moving and unmoving, is through the vajra prasena of Great Bliss. Accomplishing the knowledge by which one's own body, speech, and mind see, hear, speak, and teach by the vajra is through meditating on oneself as the vajra of Great Bliss. Bringing the hearing consciousness fully to engage all the various letters, or all the various things, of the past, the future, and the present that are seen as the command, is through the vajra omniscience of Great Bliss. Turning back all lightning, rain, and thunderbolts, and dispersing clouds, is through the wrathful vajra gaze of Great Bliss. Bringing forth and taking up, from everything beneath the earth, crops and other fruits that had not appeared before; bringing forth and taking up water from places without water; generating and taking up great light in great darkness; commanding and taking up all winds; and seeing and taking up from all of space whatever things the mind desires, are through the Great Bliss Vajradhātvīśvarī. Destroying all, paralyzing, tearing apart, stupefying, putting to sleep, sealing, felling, confounding, slaying, and turning back, are through the vicious vajra mudrā of Great Bliss. The practice of performing all rites, of passion for all, summoning, bringing in, bringing down possession, binding, releasing, tearing apart, felling, raising up what has fallen, leading, enslaving, dispatching and commanding, drawing all near and sending them away, slaying and reviving again, and the like, and of bringing under control, is through the vajra accomplishment of all of Great Bliss. The extensive rite for swiftly accomplishing all the supreme accomplishments, the supreme accomplishment of all the families and the rest; all the supreme empowerments and the rest; all excellences; the bliss of passion for all and the like; fully instilling all happiness of mind; and all acts such as all the great offerings, is through the supreme vajra accomplishment of Great Bliss.

Of these, this is the tantra of the karma mudrā of the first supreme pledge of the pledge of the vajra secret of Great Bliss. The vajra of Great Bliss gives the life of Vajrasattva. All its karma mudrās are to be bound. One should drink the middle vajra. Making light rays from its tips, one thoroughly purifies the face and should release it at the face. Having circled it all around oneself, one should release it at the heart. One circles the uṣṇīṣa, all the directions, and the maṇḍala.

Placing it at the heart, and with the mind at the back, the shoulders, the crown and the forehead, one binds the garland with the two vajra fists and puts on the armor. Having tied a knot, one again makes the garland at the vajra places, ties a knot at the back, and lets the ribbons hang down. They should be placed at the heart, the forehead, the throat and the crown. This is the supreme silk of the middle one.

For the activity there is piercing with the opening turned upward, and gazing with a terrifying face. Casting it in the manner of the four blazes and binding it at one's own wrathful heart, one should seize the tips with a peaceful gaze. One should seize with the gaze. One should seize with the gaze. By lowering it, one should swiftly release it. Having circled it all around, one places it at the forehead. When the two tips of the middle vajra fingers are moved, one places it, just as it is, at the heart of the one who performs the action.

The actions of the middle vajra are these: at the heart, raising, generating, binding, making it dance, clearing the throat, pressing the vajra tips, turning it face down, clearing the throat, swiftly releasing what is bound, protecting, and the actions of activity. At the heart, union is generated.

Each day, once in the early morning, there is thoroughly purifying the body, speech with a cleared throat, placing at the heart, and gazing at the mudrā: each day, once in the early morning, one should circle it around all the senses. What is done with the middle vajra is done with the gaze of the wrathful vajra.

The actions of the middle vajra arise from below: enjoying, summoning everything, granting descent, making blazing sparks, taking up, the manner of the wind, and meditating in space. These are many actions.

As for the body of the middle vajra, these are its actions: destroying with the mudrā, pressing the two tips at the heart, rubbing the nails, raising the eyes and closing them, lifting it to the heart with the fingers held even, letting it fall and raising it up, striking one against the other, and placing it in its natural state at one's own heart.

The actions of the middle vajra are these: aiming the arrow, summoning with the two chains, inserting the right into the left, moving the tips, binding it as a bracelet, swiftly releasing, swiftly tearing apart with the fingers held even, letting it fall, lifting it from below, drawing the left with the right, applying it to all actions, moving it from below and drawing it upward, drawing it inward and sending it outward, lifting forcefully with left and right, drawing the tips to one's own heart, the action of definitively applying it to all actions, setting the wheel at the heart, binding the garland and ribbons of empowerment, drawing out the action at the heart and drawing in bliss, thoroughly purifying everything, and taking it up with a dance and releasing it at the crown.

For the full accomplishment of Great Bliss
And for the purity of great passion,
The tantra of the mudrās, unfailing, unobstructed and manifold,
Has been thoroughly explained.

By the great Vajra Holder,
The very self of all the buddhas with their heirs,
So that all sentient beings may swiftly attain,
All unfailing accomplishments are bestowed.

Those who have not entered the maṇḍalas
And are possessed of every evil deed,
Even they will attain accomplishment,
For by merely binding it their evil is purified.

Whoever, through laziness,
Or though possessed of diligence, is deficient,
Whatever beings have no maṇḍala,
And those not blessed by a master,
They become great vajras of supreme accomplishment
And become vajra masters themselves.
By merely binding the supreme, unfailing and manifold,
They swiftly attain,
And enter into all the supreme accomplishments
That fulfill every wish.

The supreme vajra that arises first
Arises truly from Great Bliss,
Pure and perfectly accomplished,
And for all beings as well.

Next, the secret tantra for accomplishing all the mudrās:

One who desires the rite should first, with the mudrā,
Accomplish it through the heart mantra according to one's fortune,
And should definitely apply it to oneself.

From the great king of ritual manuals, the Vajra Secret of Great Bliss, the extensive rite of the later tantra called the Supreme Unfailing Accomplishment of the Vajra Pledge of All Great Bliss is complete.

Then, concerning the application of the actions of all the vajras of the body, speech and mind of the Blessed One, of all the tathāgatas, which are utterly without final limit, they, together with the Vajradharas, entreated Vajrasattva, who is without beginning or end, that through this great king of ritual manuals, the Vajra Secret Pledge of Great Bliss, there be the extensive rite of pervading and emanating throughout the entire realm of sentient beings and of all accomplishments, for the sake of the scripture that brings to its culmination the culmination of the peak of all existence:

Through the great vajra space
Comes the supreme emanation of the dharmadhātu;
The great vajra space
Grants the accomplishment of emanating the dharmadhātu.
All the buddhas, at all times,
Together with the great Vajradhara,
Are unable to bring
The vehicle of Vajradhara to its final limit.
How wonderful! For beings who cling,
For the lazy and those burdened with every karma,
You, vajra of holy compassion,
Are the unsurpassed awakening mind.
For the full accomplishment of Great Bliss
And for the purity of great passion,
That this ritual manual, the culmination,
May be fully accomplished, proclaim the culmination.

Then the Blessed One, having heard the words of entreaty made by all the tathāgatas, entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called the Blessing of Vajrasattva, the meditative absorption of all the tathāgatas, and spoke this:

For the full accomplishment of Great Bliss
And for the purity of great passion,
For the sake of accomplishing all intentions,
The culmination is blessed.

Those who will hear this supreme way,
And those in whose house all secrets abide,
To them, in this very life, is shown the accomplishment
Of the supreme secret, the supreme vajra of supreme Great Bliss.
Because it is steadfast, it does not itself impose discipline.
For the sake of beings, since the Victors are pure of karma,
This is what brings about purification.
This ritual manual has no obstructors and no māras;
It is blessed so that accomplishment cannot fail to increase.
I do not bring about Māra or instability;
Because it is steadfast, it does not itself impose discipline.
Whoever practices this supreme great ritual manual
Swiftly reaches supreme accomplishment and becomes steadfast in himself.
Steadfast and well performed, he is loved by the Steadfast One,
And Great Bliss, the maker of all, flourishes greatly.
Whoever practices my first heart mantra,
To him I always grant accomplishment.

Then all the tathāgatas assembled, gave their "Excellent!" and praised what the Blessed One had spoken.

The later tantra of supreme accomplishment of the king of ritual manuals called the Vajra Secret of Great Bliss is complete.

Then the Blessed One once again revealed this very great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, smiling broadly. Brandishing with vajra pride the vajra of Great Bliss, the vajra of the supreme first, and holding it at his own heart in the manner of praise, he spoke the great later tantra of the Maheśvara king of ritual manuals called the First Supreme Weapon: this supremely secret and supremely accomplished pledge, the Unfailing and Unimpeded, the unfailing vajra of Great Bliss, which, by the power of the blessing that tames all conceptual thought, evil, obscurations and all māras, causes even those who are unaccomplished to accomplish the holy supreme, and which is supremely secret and without beginning or end.

Then the first abode, delightful,
Stands throughout all of space,
Beautiful with the light of jewels, brilliantly clear,
Adorned with a stainless luster.
It is square, with four doors,
Beautifully adorned with porches,
Encircled all around by a parapet,
And worshiped by hosts of vajra goddesses.
In all the corner directions
And at the junctions of the porches
Half-moons are set.
With blazing vajra jewels,
Supreme pinnacles stirred by the wind,
Banners and pennants, bells that ring out,
Nets, mirrors, crests and flowers,
It is encircled with ornaments of flower garlands.
Great Bliss, the great vajra,
Well fashioned by all the vajras,
Is the great dwelling place of Vajrasattva,
Frequented by all the buddhas,
The supreme abode of bodhisattvas,
The abode of all the Vajra Holders.
The great vajra of Great Bliss,
The great Vajradhara called the supreme Vajraratna,
Having taken his seat there,
Abides in Great Bliss.

The being without beginning or end
Is Vajrasattva, of great joy,
Samantabhadra, the self of all,
Lord of lords in the manner of vajra pride.
That which is the mind of all sentient beings,
Because it is steadfast, is called heroic.
Being firmly placed, it is meditative absorption;
From certainty, it becomes vajra.
Through the yoga of blessing it as the being,
One becomes Vajrasattva himself.
He himself is the manifold Blessed One,
In accord with the conduct of awakening;
He is Māra and the victor over Māra;
He is the awakened Buddha himself.
He is the fierce vajra, Antaka himself,
And Vajrapāṇi himself as well.
He is the tathāgata family;
He is the great vajra family;
He is the pure lotus family;
He is declared the excellent jewel family.
He is free of attachment;
He is desire and liberation.
He is the three times, the three existences, the three supreme ones,
Supreme in the three worlds, belonging to the three realms;
He is the immovable, the supreme of all;
The firm teaching, the great wealth;
The moving, compounded by mind;
The maker of all, and all things themselves.
The first being, the great being,
The bodhisattva, bestows Supreme Bliss.
Seated in the cross-legged posture of the hero
Upon a lotus,
With a manifold host of consorts,
He is surrounded by vajra goddesses.

For the accomplishment of Great Bliss,
For the purity of great desire,
For the sake of all who are to be tamed, without exception,
And for the full accomplishment of all accomplishments,
The supreme pledge, in its entirety,
Should be guarded even by the Victors.
Brandishing the vajra with pride
And holding the posture of vajra pride,
So that all mudrās are swiftly and directly accomplished,
For the supreme accomplishment of Vajrasattva,
It is the great means of all accomplishments,
Greatly beheld through all merits,
Fully endowed with the wisdom mudrā:
It is the great and holy heart mantra.
Even one who has no merit,
Or likewise one who has not entered a maṇḍala,
Becomes supreme, unfailing and unimpeded;
All accomplishments are hard to find.
Great Bliss, the great vajra,
The steadfast pledge of the supreme secret,
Grants the accomplishment of all things
And swiftly accomplishes all ritual manuals.
Because it is without beginning or end, it arises first;
It is declared to be the supreme and the first.
With all words, whatever one wishes,
And with all passions, whatever delights,
Saying, "I shall be passionately devoted,"
The wisdom that descends just as it is enters.
The heart mantra, together with the near-heart mantra,
Was spoken by the Blessed One in the first eon:
hūṃ

Then, merely by his speaking this,
All the buddhas with their heirs,
Together with the manifold Vajra Holders,
Assembled in an instant.
First, through the great Vajra Holder,
The three worlds arise in a moment.
"How are beings fully accomplished?"
Thus the sugatas asked about accomplishment.
Having heard their words,
The Vajra Holder, himself liberated,
Acting as producer, bestower and teacher,
Respectfully spoke these words:
"The worlds tamed by those free of attachment
Are purified by freedom from attachment itself.
Great desire, great bliss:
How will this be accomplished?"
Having heard those words of his,
The Victor himself declared:
"That awakening mind is the first deity;
You are great means itself."

"As long as the supreme sage
Remains in the abode of saṃsāra,
So long, unequaled, he is able to work
For the benefit of beings without passing into nirvāṇa.
As long as beings have conceptual thought,
So long is awakening a great existence.
For the sake of awakening and to benefit beings,
Vajra Bearer, do as you please."

Then the first, the maṇḍala,
The first maṇḍala, was blessed:
The supreme pledge of all the vajras,
Accomplished through the rite just as it is.
Then, for the accomplishment of Great Bliss
And for the purity of great desire,
The chief entreated the protector, that Blessed One:
"Blessed One, I entreat you:
Great desire, great bliss,
This heart mantra, the supreme ritual manual,
Swiftly accomplishes great bliss."
Having heard their words,
Vajrasattva, the great bliss,
The great vajra of Great Bliss,
Declared the great maṇḍala.

Now what is to be explained
Is the holy great maṇḍala,
The supreme vajra of Great Bliss,
Declared to be the holy secret.
First, the vajra master
Who has obtained the three pledges, with his own mudrā,
In the rites of all the wheels,
Should always hold the vajra and bell.
They bring down all the ritual manuals correctly;
They accomplish all the maṇḍalas.
This seal, called the three great pledges,
Accomplishes everything.
In all the rites of accomplishment,
By vigorously ringing the vajra bell,
Even if the rite has something extra or is incomplete,
It will be accomplished; it should not be disparaged.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

The supreme first, infinite and supreme,
Unsurpassed and unequaled,
Abiding throughout all of space,
Is the supreme, the holy syllable.
Having purified the site of the maṇḍala,
In its center, according to the rite,
Through the rite of blessing for accomplishment,
That maṇḍala should be accomplished.
With the rite of songs and music,
With offerings of flowers, incense and the like,
As many as there are and as one wishes,
One should always do this for the supreme first.

In the supreme first, infinite and supreme,
Unsurpassed and incomparable,
Abiding throughout all of space,
Is the supreme unchanging city,
Without beginning, supreme and permanent,
Where the arising of the buddhas also abides.
Resting on the equality of the all-supreme,
Without excess or lack, filling all,
Is space without edge or center.
Examining this as the middle,
Through the meditative absorption of the yoga of mudrā,
By that it is examined as the center.
In its center, the form of a lotus
With its stalk should be set,
Covered all over with manifold lotuses:
That is the seat of total purity.
Then the lines should be struck
With a cord made double.
Because it is the measure of all space,
And because the minds of those to be tamed are infinite,
Its measure is measureless.
Since nothing is unequal, it is square;
Mindfulness and the faculties are the four doors;
The concentrations are the four archways;
Meditative absorption is declared the courtyard.
The lord of the vajra resting in the courtyard,
And the forms, are worshiped by goddesses.
Made brilliant by every ornament,
It fulfills every wish.
The supreme manifold, set in motion by the taming,
The holy Dharma, supreme, goes everywhere.
Banners of many kinds, stirred by the wind,
Are tied to the pinnacles, and bells resound.
The mirror is wisdom; the crests are flowers;
The limbs of awakening are the flower garlands;
Nets, mirrors and great flowers,
And it is encircled by ornaments of flower garlands.
The bliss that is all-accomplished and unchanging
Is declared to be Great Bliss.
The palace of Great Bliss
Should be drawn in the first place.
Having drawn well that supreme of all,
The supreme city of great liberation,
Contemplate the expanse without beginning or end,
With all directions as equality itself.
That is the outer maṇḍala.

The inner wheel within it
Is like an eightfold maṇḍala:
These are the eight liberations,
Which grant the bliss of all accomplishments.
By turning the wheel of the sūtras
Of the Great Vehicle of the bodhisattvas,
One correctly draws the vajra wheel,
Encircled by vajra lines.
There, upon a great manifold lotus,
Vajrasattva should be set,
Whether cast, or carved,
Or fashioned, or painted:
Smiling and the color of the moon,
Red and blazing with manifold light,
With the empowerment of all the buddhas and so forth,
With manifold ornaments and garments,
Seated in the cross-legged posture of the hero,
Supreme holder of the manner of vajra pride.
Saying surata stvaṃ,
One should also set him in place with his own heart mantra.

In all of its directions,
With colors, or likewise with powders,
In their proper order,
One should correctly draw the vajra goddesses.
One should be diligent in vajra offering and praise.
Draw Vajrarāgā in front.
The goddess Vajravilāsinī
Is closely embracing the Vajra Bearer.
Behind, Vajrasmṛti
Holds the makara banner.
Vajrakāmeśvarī
Should be drawn proudly with two vajra fists.

Then the mudrās for setting these in place are:
jaḥ vajra driṣṭi samkemaṭa hūṃ
oṃ vajra kili kili hūṃ
vaṃ vajriṇi smararaṭa
ho vajra kāmeśvari stvaṃ

In the corners, Vajrasattvā and the others,
The offerings of the seasons, should be correctly drawn:
The vajra lady of honey and the clouds,
Autumn and winter, the vajra ladies.
Flowers, incense, great lamp,
And scent, with palms joined at the crown,
Are the goddesses of the vajra king.
Then the mudrās shall be explained:
oṃ madhu vajri ghuṃ ghuṃ
oṃ vajra meghe gulu gulu
oṃ oṃ śarada vajri
oṃ oṃ
oṃ vajra śiśiri hūṃ hūṃ

The Graceful One, the Laughing One, the Great Singer,
And the Dancer, the host of goddesses,
These vajra musicians
Should be placed in the outer corners.
he rati vajra maghavilasini traha
he rati vajra nṛtyai veva veva

At all the places of the doors
Are the four door attendants:
The vajra hook, the noose,
The chain, and likewise the bell lady:
vajra aṃguśa jaḥ
vajra pāśe hūṃ
vajra śriṃkhale vaṃ
vajra ghaṇḍe hoḥ

This pledge will be contemplated
And examined by all.
The host of vajra goddesses and the rest
Will arise for all accomplishments.
Of the door attendants and the serving women,
There are chief ladies, messengers and handmaids.

Then the All-Supreme and the others Should be drawn and correctly accomplished. Having bound the supreme pledge, One should say jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ. Seated in the cross-legged posture of the hero Before the Vajra Bearer, One should once correctly summon the lord, The supreme vajra of Great Bliss: hūṃ mahāsukha vajrasatva yahi śīghraṃ mahāsukha vajra amoghāya anupālaya prabuddha surata stvaṃ anurakto me bhava sutoṣyo me bhava supoṣyo me bhava bhagavān anādinas tva sarva siddhimme prayaccha aśetamatriṣa āveśaya samyer baddha vaśīkaro me imer mudrāpaṅaiḥ jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ By this the Blessed One always Comes himself into the maṇḍala; Summoned and made to enter, he is very firm; Brought under control, he is fully won over.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Great Pledge.

The vajra master will see
His own deity displaying light rays everywhere, and so on:
Many kinds of signs
That are wondrous.
Then the vajra guest water
Is offered with the vajra palms opened.
With vases, however many are filled,
And with flowers and the like, one also makes offering.
Banners with bells tied at their tips
Should be set at the sections of the boundary.
Over a vase filled with scented water,
Blessed with the vajra,
One should recite fully over the twig.

Here, the accomplishment of the water of the pledge of the unfailing vajra of Great Bliss is this: hūṃ mahāsukha vajra amogha samayodaka saddha abhiṣiñca sarva sukha saumanasyānyādada kuru kuru jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ Having recited this fully twenty-one times, one should empower oneself with the vajra. Sprinkling it for purification, and drinking it as well, will remove all suffering and increase all great bliss. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

Then, even though he has not entered the wheel,
He himself enters first,
And the great master of all the vajras
Blesses himself through the Vajra Bearer.
Then the master, the Vajra Holder,
Bathes himself and anoints himself well with scents,
Adorns himself with all the ornaments there are,
Wears garments of deep red,
Fastens on a flower garland and scents his mouth.
With the vajra of graceful joy
And the yoga of the vajra dance,
Joining his palms with flowers at the crown,
He should pay homage to that place, the protector.
Having entered that maṇḍala,
He should circumambulate it three times.
Then, sitting in front,
He should recite the suchness once:
"Great desire, great bliss,
Great vajra, great wealth,
Great wisdom, great action:
These are declared the accomplishment of Vajrasattva."
Then, scattering loose flowers
On the protector according to the rite,
He should sing once, as a song,
The praise beginning with śara.

Then, the great suchness, the king of praises of the Blessed One, of one hundred and eight names, is this:

Śrī Paramādya, the great being,
The great joy of great joy,
Samantabhadra, the self of all,
Lord of lords in the manner of vajra pride;
Mind, and the supreme meditative absorption of the being,
Vajra of vajras, great and supreme,
The supreme conduct of Samantabhadra,
Utter destroyer of Māra and the māras;
All of all, the great awakening,
Buddha of buddhas, supreme born of the born,
Vajrahūṃkāra, the syllable hūṃ,
Lokeśvara, brilliant wealth;
Great desire, great bliss itself,
Desire and liberation, the great wealth,
The three times, the three existences, the three supreme ones,
Supreme in the three worlds, belonging to the three realms;
Steadfast, self-arisen and luminous,
Very subtle, gross and well gathered,
The moving one who attains the very supreme,
Who purifies existence and saṃsāra,
Without beginning or end, yet having beginning and end,
Radiant, abiding in all from the first;
Heart mantra, mudrā, yoga and pledge,
Suchness, the supreme truth,
The great accomplishment of the tathāgatas,
Dharma and action, the great element,
The holy Dharma, lord of supreme action,
Who thoroughly purifies the awakening mind;
Vajrakrodha, the great wrathful one,
The tamer blazing like the end of time,
The great tamer, supreme over the vicious,
The fierce one who consumes the fierce;
The all-pure great lotus,
Wisdom and means, the great way,
The supreme meditative absorption of purified desire,
Manifold passion, Maheśvara;
Infinite space, the meaning of permanence,
The great abode of all beings,
All riches, the abode of riches,
Glory, chief and king,
Who fulfills every wish:
Homage, homage to you!
Homage, I bow, homage!
With faith I rely on you;
Vajrasattva, grant me accomplishment!

Whoever recites this king of praises
Every morning,
Or says it once with words of Dharma,
Will gain accomplishment very swiftly.
If one does not wish for accomplishment here,
Then, first, praising it each day at the three times,
One is surely freed from evil
And accomplishes a pure field.
It always removes all suffering.
By reciting it fully once,
One makes all accomplishments fortunate
And increases excellent glory.
Endowed with the qualities of unfailing accomplishment
Are these holy one hundred and eight names.

Then, with the secret offering,
Greatly delighted, one makes offering to the protector.
Once, with one's own mudrā,
Binding one's own mudrā, one accomplishes it.
Then, with the water of the deity,
And likewise with the supreme body, one confers empowerment.
For oneself, in the vajra mudrā,
One should take up this heart mantra.

Here the heart mantra of empowerment as Vajrasattva is this: hūṃ mahāsukha vajrasatva abhiṣṭekena hrīḥ abhiṣiñcāmi sarva vajra adhipaji stvi dṛḍho me bhava midhanāmamaste bhagavāna mahāvajradhara surata stvaṃ mahāvajrasatva oṃ bhur bhuva sva

Then, empowered with the vajra,
Holding the eye of Vajrapāṇi,
One becomes fully accomplished, right here,
As the master, the great Vajra Holder.
Then also, as one wishes,
Having accomplished the holy rite,
And having made fine offerings
With the offering of the vajra dance, one should come out.
From then on, the lord of the vajra,
Holder of the scriptures of all the vajras,
The master who holds all the vajras,
Will accomplish all accomplishments.
Vajrasattva, the great accomplishment,
Lord of all mudrās, Maheśvara:
dhīḥ
The great vajra of omniscience:
All actions are to be examined in the center.
Unshakable and invincible,
Maker of all, he grants all accomplishments.
The great king of all beings,
He is able to subdue and to benefit.
Supreme of all, he makes all devoted,
All-knowing and the abode of all,
Fully engaged in every desire,
The great lord of every excellence.
All beings, gods and the rest,
Always carry out his every command.
He always sees all the beings,
And attains accomplishment in this very life.
Through that very thing he is endowed with desire;
Joyful, he himself becomes the lord,
The vajra master.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

This is the tantra of the extensive rite of blessing and entry for the vajra master of Great Bliss.

Then the Blessed One Vajrasattva said, "Next I shall explain the tantra of the extensive rite for the entry of the disciple."

Circling the vajra guest water at the heart,
Having bound the mudrā, as the thought stirs,
One bows down properly with the head:
This accomplishes all accomplishments.
Having entered the maṇḍala
And circumambulated it three times,
One declares, "Great desire, great bliss
Brings accomplishment to all beings as well."
Then, with the offerings of the seasons,
One makes proper offering to Great Bliss.
Likewise, for empowerment,
The disciple of the vajra secret should be brought in.
Then, having adorned him fittingly,
The vajra master leads him in.
Having brought in the hosts of disciples,
They obtain whatever accomplishment they wish.
Then, having brought the disciple in,
One should say these words of great joy:
"You who reveal great delight and great bliss,
Vajrasattva, lord, grant me accomplishment today!"
Then the flower garland
Is swiftly cast down from the crown of the head.
Wherever the flower falls,
In that chosen deity one becomes fully accomplished.
Then, with the water of the deity,
One empowers the Vajra Holder and oneself.
Giving the great vajra, the five vajras,
One declares the vajra bodhi:
oṃ mahāsukha vajra vajra abhiṣekena staṃ abhiśiñcāmi mahāvajradhāra he vajranāma siddha nāmaḥ

Whatever a person's vajra name is, it should be joined with the vocative he, meaning "O." Then, having given the heart mantra and taught the binding of the mudrā, one should accomplish whatever one desires.

Whether it is the rite of Vajrasattva
Or whatever one wishes for one's own accomplishment,
For that, through that very deity, the pledge itself
And the rites of entry and the rest are explained.
The various worldly deities,
And likewise the transcendent ones,
All of them, each in its own order,
If practiced extensively, will be fully accomplished.
By merely entering into this,
One becomes a child of the Victors,
Unshakable and irreversible,
And it grants the full accomplishment of Great Bliss.
Then the master, the Vajra Holder,
Having solemnly proclaimed the oath,
Should train himself and others
In suchness, the Dharma and the pledge.

Here, having empowered the disciple with the vajra water that seals the oath, one should say this:
"This is your water of hell:
If you transgress the pledge, it burns;
If you guard the pledge, you will be accomplished:
It is the vajra nectar water of accomplishment.
As for suchness, the Dharma and the pledge,
If you speak of them to one who has not seen the maṇḍala,
Some people who stay in gatherings,
Having no faith, will laugh at them."

Here, this is the full bestowal of the secret rite of suchness and the pledge:
The being without beginning or end
Is Vajrasattva, of great joy,
Samantabhadra, the self of all,
Lord of lords in the manner of vajra pride.

One should declare the explanation of the great suchness of the five secrets of supreme suchness. The Blessed One spoke that very supreme Dharma: Existence is pure by its essential nature; By its essential nature it is made free of existence. The supreme being, pure in intrinsic nature, Is what makes existence supreme.

Here, the method of purifying existence, the supreme secret of all ritual manuals, the great pledge to be relied upon, is this: Because the bliss of existence is very slight, And to purify the great suffering, The method of holy, supreme bliss, The pledge, should always be relied upon. While relying on all the enjoyments of desire Just as one wishes, Through the yoga of one's own chosen deity One should make offering to oneself and to others.

Here one should declare this great pledge of the supreme secret of all ritual manuals:
As long as the supreme sage
Remains in the abode of saṃsāra,
So long, unequaled, he is able to work
For the benefit of beings without passing into nirvāṇa.
The method of the pledge of the supreme secret,
Which accomplishes merit and blessing,
Supreme lord of all accomplishments,
Works continually for the benefit of beings.
Thus make your mind clear and vajra itself;
Rely on your own inexhaustible pledge of bliss and joy.
That will swiftly be realized for beings as bliss,
Fully equal to all the buddhas, and as permanence itself.

Then, having duly presented offerings of elixirs, food, lodging, songs, music and the like, you should honor the great vajra master even with your very self, to whatever extent is possible.

Having duly honored the vajra master with flowers and the like, as one wishes,
And having worshipped him rightly with the offering of the vajra dance, one comes forth.

Then the Vajra Holder, the master,
Having been honored with the offering of dance,
Is fully honored by all the disciples
With the joining of the vajra palms.
With elixirs, food, lodging,
Songs and music, as much as one wishes,
Having rightly honored Vajrasattva, and likewise oneself,
One should then come forth.

This is the supremely secret maṇḍala called Śrī Paramādya.

Next the practice shall be explained,
The accomplishment that is utterly permanent,
Through which, in this very existence,
One attains permanent accomplishment.
Through this, bodhisattvas,
Abiding in existence and awakened within existence,
Accomplish lordship over all accomplishments
And always act for the benefit of all beings.
The supreme accomplishment as it is gained through this
Is not gained in that way through the conduct of awakening and the like,
Nor through other virtuous ways,
Not even by all the buddhas and the rest.
For those unfit to be trained, who lack merit,
The buddhas and vajra holders,
Through being seen and so forth, in every way,
Are unable to act for beings' benefit.
Therefore one accomplished through the great tantra,
Whether a buddha or a vajra holder,
Through being seen and so forth acts for the benefit of beings
To an extent equal to space.
Great passion, great bliss,
The stainless certainty of existence,
Acts for the benefit of beings boundless as space,
Without passing into nirvāṇa.
Even one who has not entered the maṇḍalas,
Or who lacks merit, or who is lazy,
Through the accomplishment of the All-Supreme
Will attain accomplishment in this very life.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Amoghasiddhi Vajrasattva.

Then the Blessed One Vajrasattva said, "Next I shall explain the supreme swift accomplishment of the Unfailing and Unimpeded."

Having fashioned the First Being,
Whether cast, or carved,
Or made, or painted,
One should practice in front of it.
Each day, at the first session,
Likewise endowed with the sense objects,
Summoning with the first supreme secret,
One should fully bless the great mudrā.
Gazing likewise with the vajra eye,
In the vajra manner of pride, oneself the lord,
Singing the vajra hūṃ,
One should accomplish Vajrasattva.
With the vajra attire just as prescribed,
The supreme First One is greatly delighted.
First the image and so forth,
As one wishes, should be fully accomplished.
Through this one becomes lord of all desires,
By mere wish one goes, comes and soars through the sky;
One accomplishes lordship over all accomplishments,
And is the lord of the sublime All-Supreme.
Lord of all perfections, of everything,
Great passion, great bliss,
Who causes all to enter, manifold,
Ever taming everyone.
Supreme bliss of all accomplishments,
Lord and master of all mudrās,
Great accomplishment of all vajras,
Great source of omniscience,
Unshakable and indestructible,
Doer of all and abode of all,
Victorious over the three realms, manifold,
Subduing and benefiting,
Loving toward all, supreme of all,
All-knowing, bestowing every accomplishment,
In every world-system
He displays his own magical emanations.
The All-Supreme, the great accomplishment,
The great lord of Maheśvara,
Supreme vajra of form, life and strength,
Like a second Vajrasattva,
Worshipped and praised by beings,
Relied upon by his own kin,
Like the king, the great Vajradhara,
He becomes free of aging and death.
If one wishes to become Vajrasattva,
Or a buddha, or a bodhisattva itself,
Whatever accomplishment one desires
Will be accomplished in this very life.
In later lives, or in worldly ways,
By the rites taught in all the ritual manuals,
Or by those one composes oneself, always
All accomplishments will be accomplished.
Thus spoke the Blessed One, the vajra
Lord of accomplishments, Great Bliss,
The great king Vajradhara,
The All-Supreme, lord of the earth.

How, then, is the tantra for accomplishing one's own mudrā?
Resting in equipoise in the great mudrā,
Accomplish it once with one's own heart mantra:
dṛḍha sutoṣya saṃrakta supo siddha talagu

How, then, is the tantra of singing the syllable hūṃ?
Adorned with ridhita and the like,
As far as one is able and as it pleases,
Through the syllable hūṃ, sung with gaṇḍe and the like,
Accomplishment will come.

Then the tantra for accomplishing
The supreme of all mudrās is explained.
If one wishes to accomplish the mudrā,
Likewise endowed with the sense objects,
Binding the seal oneself,
One should practice it tirelessly.

How, then, is the tantra for accomplishing all pledges?
Having bound the pledge mudrā,
Saying stvaṃ with the mind,
At one's own heart, in due order,
One should accomplish that supreme mudrā.

Then the tantra for accomplishing the supreme action mudrā:
If one practices tirelessly
With the offering of the vajra dance,
The activities, all offerings and the rest,
Will be accomplished in this very life.
The state of the great Vajradhara,
Buddhahood and bodhisattvahood,
And moreover all accomplishments,
The sublime ones, will swiftly be accomplished.

This is the extensive tantra of the rite of the great practice of all ritual procedures, unfailingly accomplished.

Then the tantra of the accomplishments of practicing all ritual procedures: "For a single day, intensely, accomplishing the mudrā once, I shall explain the unfailing and unimpeded accomplishment," said the Blessed One.

For accomplishing the delighting of all, summoning, binding, and bringing under one's control, it is by the vajra gaze of great bliss. For increasing bliss, mental happiness, prosperity and all accomplishments, it is by the vajra Dharma of great bliss. For increasing life, youth, form and great splendor, it is by the rejuvenating elixir of the first vajra. For increasing great glory, good fortune, renown and fame, it is by the accomplishment of the first vajra. For accomplishing great strength, wisdom, Dharma and action, it is by the great courage of the first vajra. For displaying magical powers, miracles, wonders and all manner of emanations, it is by the magical emanation of the first vajra.

For accomplishing everywhere the seeing, hearing, speaking and questioning of all the truths of letters and of forms, it is by the unfailing truth of the first vajra. For seeing, hearing, speaking and overpowering all truths with all the senses, it is by the unfailing truth of the first vajra. For accomplishing all illusions, supreme bliss, oracular commands, and the obtaining of answers, it is by the marvel of the first vajra. For extracting all treasures, seizing wealth, gaining a kingdom, and enjoying the earth, it is by the empowerment of the first vajra. For emanating all lands, peoples, cities, forests and rivers to be enjoyed, it is by the accomplishment of the first vajra. For emanating and enjoying divine realms surpassing all and never before seen, jewels, food, drink, servants, retinues and the like, it is by the emanation of the first vajra. For causing the whole earth in an instant to ripen grain and the like, bringing forth great masses of water, producing great light, and bringing all the winds under one's control, it is by the first vajra, the Lord of the Sphere. For eating and drinking all things moving and unmoving, making them one's playthings, and employing them in all activities, it is by the unfailing all-subjugation of the first vajra. For seeing, among all accomplishments, the tathāgatas, bodhisattvas, vajra holders, vajra beings and the rest, it is by the pride of the first vajra. For emanating and displaying oneself in every form, such as Vajrasattva, accomplishing invisibility and travel through the sky, and appearing as extremely subtle, as gross, and as manifold, it is by the manifold forms of the first vajra.

For bringing down, subjugating and binding all who are lords of the three worlds, and employing them in all activities, it is by the first Vajrahūṃkāra. For bringing down, binding and subjugating all the gods, and definitively employing them in all activities, it is by the first vajra, Lord of the Gods. For exorcising, taming and subjugating all the nāgas, and definitively employing them in all activities, it is by the first vajra, the Blazing Vajra. For summoning and subjugating all the yakṣas and the rest, definitively employing them in all activities, and terrifying them, it is by the first vajra, Tamer of Grahas. For examining, entering, worshipping and practicing all maṇḍalas, and definitively employing them in all activities, it is by the first vajra wheel. For summoning, binding, subjugating, stabilizing and accomplishing all mudrās, it is by the mudrā accomplishment of the first vajra. For summoning, causing to enter, binding and accomplishing all pledges, it is by the supreme pledge of the first vajra. For fully accomplishing all activities, for making fearless, making stable, and proclaiming great renown, it is by the action of the first vajra. For accomplishing the summoning, causing to enter, binding and subjugation of all vajras, it is by the first vajra. For accomplishing the summoning, causing to enter, binding and subjugation of all great beings, it is by the first Vajrasattva. For accomplishing the summoning, causing to enter, binding and subjugation of all the tathāgatas, it is by the first Tathāgata. And it was also said: for summoning, causing to enter, binding and subjugating the great Vajra Holder, it is by the first Vajra Holder.

Even one who has not entered the maṇḍalas,
Or who has committed every evil,
Or who has not practiced his own heart mantra,
Will accomplish all accomplishments.
Without practice, all is accomplished.

This is the supreme accomplishment of the great mudrā, unfailingly accomplished.

Then the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, the manifold activity of all vajras, said, "Next I shall explain the instantaneous accomplishment of the cause of all activities, unfailing and unimpeded in everything, without practice of the first heart mantra."

Unfailing and unimpeded,
I, together with the sugatas,
Accomplish these vast activities
As accomplishments in a single instant.

Namely: for thoroughly protecting and guarding all, pacifying, bringing well-being, and increasing life, it is by the uṣṇīṣa mudrā in the manner of the being of the vajra dance. For binding and protecting the boundaries, directions, pledges and accomplishments of all maṇḍalas, it is by the great Pratisarā of the vajra dance. For dispelling tertian fever, quartan fever, recurrent fever, chronic fever, and all the fevers of Viṣṇu, it is by the freedom from fever of the vajra dance. For destroying all torments, harm-doers, illnesses, sorrows and sufferings, it is by the pacification of suffering of the vajra dance. For complete freedom from poison and from compounded poison, and for bringing it down, binding it, eating it, and turning it into nectar, it is by the secondary poison of the vajra dance. For bringing down all grahas, binding them, making them hold to the pledge, fully taming them, and for protection, it is by the liberation from grahas of the vajra dance. For binding, freeing, tearing apart, felling and raising up all, it is by the great vajra wrathful one of the vajra dance. For destroying the mudrās of all ḍākinīs, freeing from grahas, raising the dead, increasing life, and thorough protection, it is by the counter-mudrā of the vajra dance. For destroying all obscurations, all evils, bad dreams, bad omens and calamities, it is by the pacification of the vajra dance. For bringing down and possessing all, making them shake and dance, making all speak the truth, and accomplishing all prasen divinations, it is by the all-possessing mudrā of the vajra dance. For sealing, killing, felling, raising up and reviving all, it is by the great ḍākinī of the vajra dance of Amoghasiddhi. For paralyzing, stupefying, putting to sleep, killing and devouring all, it is by the vicious rākṣasa of the vajra dance. For terrifying, destroying, subjugating, felling and killing all enemy armies, it is by the executioner of the vajra dance.

For clearing away all wrath and making the countenance bright, for victory in disputes, liberation from fear, and victory in battle, it is by the armlet of the supreme victory banner of the vajra dance. For gathering up all clouds completely and making them unmoving, for bringing down great rain, for averting wind, rain, lightning and hail, and for dispelling heat and cold, it is by the vajra of Indra of the vajra dance. For the complete accomplishment of all forms, sounds, smells, tastes and tangibles, it is by the Lord of Desire of the vajra dance. For enamoring, summoning, subjugating and sporting with all that moves and does not move, and definitively employing them in all activities, it is by the all-accomplishment of the vajra dance. For bringing down all the gods, subjugating all the nāgas, and definitively employing all the yakṣas, bhūtas and mātṛkās in activities, it is by the supreme vajra body of the vajra dance. For suppressing Vaiśravaṇa and drawing forth his wealth, carrying off all the daughters of the gods, sporting with all the women of the gods and asuras, conquering the three worlds without exception, conquering all lords of desire, and carrying off all the Maheśvaras, it is by the great Maker of Men of the vajra dance. For accomplishing all offerings, making all into vajra, utterly destroying Māra and all the rest, binding all mudrās, and swiftly accomplishing the cause of the activities of all accomplishments, the supreme accomplishment and the rest, entirely and without remainder, it is by the sublime accomplishment of the vajra dance's manifold activities, unfailing, unimpeded and swift.

Then the extensive tantra of the rite of accomplishing all mudrās:
Each day, once at the first session,
One fully accomplishes all mudrās.
Having recited the supreme heart mantra once,
One should fully accomplish the great mudrā.
After that, through definitively applying
The secondary heart mantra to all activities,
By Amoghasiddhi the activities
Will be fully accomplished in every case.
All mudrās are fully blessed.
The mudrā of Vajrapāṇi
Will be fully accomplished even by one who has not seen the maṇḍalas,
And even by one to whom it has not been explained.
In this rite there is no fear.
For Śakra, who has no wheel,
Since he is blessed by me,
Becomes the cause of my own son.
Even to one who commits great evil,
Once his intention is known, it should be given.
Merely binding these purifies,
And all accomplishments will be accomplished.

This is the extensive ritual chapter on the pledge of the unfailing vajra of great bliss, from the great king of rites, Śrī Paramādya.

Then the Teacher, the Vajra Holder,
The producer, Great Bliss,
In order to establish beings firmly in great bliss
And to purify all sentient beings,
Equal to all realms and inexhaustible,
Entered into the nature of all,
And emanating the supreme accomplishment of equality,
Brought forth the family of the tathāgatas.
All the infinite fields,
Together with all the world-systems,
Were then swiftly filled by all the buddhas,
Like a pod full of sesame seeds.
In all the world-systems
They abided fully before all beings.
Since all beings were impure,
They did not become pure through their own awareness and actions.
Then, because the realms of beings
Are by their nature hard to heal,
In order that they purify their own karma,
The buddhas assumed great equanimity.
Then the Blessed One, the Vajra Bearer,
The great being, Great Bliss,
His mind steadfast in great awakening,
In an instant exhorted them:
"You who benefit beings, possessed of merit,
Steadfast in the mind of awakening,
Since the realm of beings is falling into ruin,
Do not remain in such utter equanimity!"
Then the buddhas said,
"Vajra Bearer of body, speech and mind,
Supreme mind of awakening, you must speak
The method of the one whose nature is compassion!"
Then the Teacher, the Vajra Holder,
At that time blessed everything:
Great bliss, the great vajra,
Is the great and sublime maṇḍala.
Through the whole expanse of the three realms of saṃsāra
The great wheel arose from the very beginning.
Through its abiding in the three realms,
All beings were fully accomplished;
In the accomplishment of all the buddhas and the rest,
Purity will then be accomplished.
Then all the buddhas
Assembled and made supplication:
"Great family of the tathāgatas,
Supreme of all buddhas, please explain!"
He, abiding fully in the body,
Speech and mind of all the buddhas,
Emanating fully into the vajra of body, speech and mind,
Uttered this: oṃ bhū khaṃ
Then by the mere utterance of this,
All the buddhas assembled,
Accomplished the conquest of Māra and the rest,
And blessed the seat of awakening.
Then the Teacher, the Vajra Holder,
Spoke the supreme of maṇḍalas,
For the sake of bliss for all beings,
The great bliss of all rites.
Next shall be fully explained
The great and sublime maṇḍala.
Outside the great maṇḍala, the supreme,
Draw a vajra wheel correctly.
At the hub of the wheel, the Buddha himself,
Smiling, his teeth not showing.
Uttering suratesamayastvaṃ
oṃ siddhamāṃ, set it in place.
In front, draw correctly the Buddha
Who brings about complete victory over Māra.
On the right, the one who confers empowerment
With the great jewel of the bodhisattvas.
Behind, the one who, together with the bodhisattvas,
Turns the wheel of Dharma.
On the left, the one who draws out all the accumulated evils
Of those who are wrongly arrogant.
Then their heart mantras are:
vajrasatva vajraratna vajradharma vajrakarma
Then the mudrās:
satvavajrī ratnavajrī padmavajrī biśvavajrī
In the corner sections draw a vajra,
A jewel tree, and a gem.
In the corners also the goddesses,
And Aṅkuśī and the others at the gates.

Likewise, just as before,
That wheel should be laid out.
Bind the palms of offering at the heart,
And likewise circle to the left.
This mudrā, together with flowers,
Is the sequence of the pledge of all the buddhas.
Together with flowers, bind it at the heart,
And, the mind stirred, one should feel longing.
Having bowed one's head to the Protector
And circumambulated three times,
Having entered, one should cast the flower,
And fully summon the utterly secret one.

Here the heart mantra for pleasing, subjugating, summoning and accomplishing all the tathāgatas is this:
oṃ akāro mukhaṃ sarvadharmāṇāṃ ādyānutbannatvāda

Then, according to the rite,
Scatter loose flowers upon the Teacher,
And in order fully to accomplish the awakening of a buddha,
Utter that very mantra to the Protector of beings.
Through that, the buddhas from every side
Will all assemble together,
And through the practice of not apprehending anything
They will bestow all accomplishments.
For one who has entered that maṇḍala
All accomplishments will be accomplished.
Supreme signs, such as joy,
Will arise in manifold ways.
Then likewise, through the extensive rite,
Lead the disciple in, in the supreme way.
Having made offering to the great wheel
With the palms of offering, lead him out again.
Having done the practice, the accomplishments too
Should be fully explained according to the rite.
On cloth draw the Blessed One
Who gained victory over Māra and the rest.
By doing this practice even once,
One will attain accomplishment through such a practice.
Before an image or the like, correctly,
Holding firmly the fist of wisdom,
With the vajra words, according to the rite,
One should perform a hundred thousand recitations.
In short, when a hundred thousand are complete,
All accomplishments one wishes,
And buddhahood itself in this supreme life,
Will be attained; of this there is no doubt.

Then the great tantra of the great practice of the great mudrā of the tathāgatas:
Having bound any one of the mudrās,
If one meditates, "I am the Buddha,"
The great accomplishment of the great mudrā,
Stable, will swiftly be attained.

Then the tantra of accomplishing the pledge:
Placing a vajra form at the heart
And saying samayastvaṃ,
Having practiced with a hundred thousand recitations,
One will attain all accomplishments.

Then the tantra of accomplishing all action mudrās:
Binding the supreme mudrā of victory over Māra,
Fully turning the wheel of Dharma,
And accomplishing the incomparable benefit of beings,
One always accomplishes the action mudrā.

Thus spoke the Blessed One. This is the extensive tantra of the rite of accomplishing the supreme accomplishment of all the tathāgatas.

Next, the supreme accomplishment without practice,
The vast and sublime activities,
Manifold, dispelling all evil,
Shall be fully explained in due order.
